# MAINTAINING — Contract → Consumer Map (FR-186)

This is the **maintained contract→consumer map** for Igris AI. A *contract* is any
token one subsystem produces that another subsystem reads: a file path, a
`table.column`, an env-var name, a `config.json` dotted key, or a protocol marker.
When a brief changes a contract, every
listed **consumer** must be re-pointed *in the same commit* — otherwise the
consumer silently breaks (the exact failure class G-01R fell through: the phase
guard kept reading the retired `CURRENT_SESSION.md` long after FR-133 moved the
Active-Brief source elsewhere, going silently inert).

## Protocol

Three layers enforce this map, each the right primitive at its layer (memory #400):

1. **Planning (architect).** The architect loads this map directly from
   `{repo_root}/MAINTAINING.md` (named in its self-contained CONTEXT PROTOCOL,
   FR-187). Any plan that changes a contract listed here MUST include a
   `## Consumer Sweep` section naming every affected consumer. An incomplete
   sweep is a rejected plan.
2. **Mechanical (pre-commit).** `scripts/check_contract_consumers.sh` parses this
   map, scans `git diff --cached` for deletions/renames of mapped tokens, and
   surfaces the consumer list. The token sweep defaults to **WARN** (it informs,
   it does not veto a legitimate refactor); a *stale map* is a **hard-fail** —
   see "Citation conventions" at the bottom of this file for exactly what that covers.
3. **Review (warden).** The §17 warden checklist row verifies each consumer in the
   changed contract's row was actually swept *correctly* in the diff — a
   reasoning judgment, not a regex.

## How to add a row

Any brief that creates a new cross-subsystem contract (a new token another
subsystem will read) **adds its row to this table in the same commit** — this is a
§13 obligation ("Runtime Contracts (Consumer-Sweep Rule)"). Keep every cell
**positive-polarity**: list what *consumes* the contract. Never phrase a cell as
"X does not consume Y" (negative-polarity cells get inverted by downstream
agents — memory #372).

The mechanical net is `scripts/check_contract_consumers.sh`. Run it standalone to preview the consumers of a changed path AND validate every citation in this file — this is the invocation that always checks the map. Arguments are **space**-separated; a comma-joined
argument is a usage error (see "Citation conventions" at the bottom of this file):

```bash
scripts/check_contract_consumers.sh --paths path/one path/two
```

## Schema

| Column | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **Contract** | The exact token a brief might change: a file path, `table.column`, env-var name, `config.json` dotted key, or protocol marker. This is the string the checker matches against staged diffs. |
| **Type** | One of `file` \| `column` \| `env-var` \| `config-key` \| `protocol` \| `line-range`. Drives how the checker greps (path-literal vs anchored identifier). |
| **Consumers (file:line)** | `<br>`-separated citations of every reader of this contract — these are the sweep targets. Both `path/to/file.ts` and `path/to/file.ts:148` are validated by the checker; read "Citation conventions" at the bottom of this file before writing one, because a token it classifies as a repo path and cannot resolve is a hard-fail. |
| **Owner brief** | The brief that introduced/owns the contract (provenance). |
| **Change procedure** | One line: what a brief changing this contract MUST do. |

What is STILL unguarded: `core/enforcement/brief-type-vocabulary.md`'s derivation table, `scripts/validate_brief_type_vocabulary.sh`'s header prefix map, and the tool-schema descriptions — all three verified unguarded by planting a corruption and getting a green suite. **TD-357** carries the six-copy census and pins the first of them. What survives of the original argument: free-form CODE COMMENTS drift visibly enough that pinning them costs more than it saves. What does not survive: applying that to STRUCTURED, RUNTIME-READ prose — `/register`'s table is executed instruction, and it shipped a live defect during TD-331 precisely because it was treated as documentation. **MINT-PREFIX CHANGE, TD-331 (operator decision 2026-08-06):** `/register` §2 now maps `bug -> BR` and `feature -> FR` as 1:1 rows, retiring the `bug, feature -> BR` collision that was the sole reason 20 briefs are untypeable (17 NULL `BR-` rows + 3 typed literally `BR`). The rule is TIGHTENED, not patched: every canonical type with a mint prefix now has exactly ONE, and `Refactor` remains the single documented prefix-less canonical type — there is no second exception. **`BR` STAYS ABSENT FROM `BRIEF_ID_PREFIX_TYPES` ANYWAY**, and the reason is not the mint rule but what that table is FOR: it decodes brief IDs that ALREADY EXIST, and all 17 NULL `BR-` rows predate the decision. Adding the key would retro-assign exactly the rows TD-331 forbids touching. The 20 are dispositioned as permanently ambiguous by decision; what TD-331 bought is that the set is CAPPED rather than growing. |
| `brief_status.priority` (the canonical VOCABULARY: `CANONICAL_PRIORITIES` and the `normalizePriority` fold). FOUR values — `P0-Critical`, `P1-High`, `P2-Medium`, `P3-Low` — plus SQL NULL, which is the *unset* family and is what the empty string folds to. UNTIL FR-247 THIS VOCABULARY HAD EXACTLY ONE SOURCE and no consumer outside the brain, so it needed no row; FR-247's dashboard picker is the FIRST out-of-brain consumer, and a picker PRESCRIBES a vocabulary rather than reporting one, so the four values are MIRRORED into the client. That mirror is the whole reason this row exists. THE CHANGE PROCEDURE, in this order: edit the brain source, mirror into the picker, re-run the picker's mirror assertion. NOTE THE DRIFT THIS ROW DOES NOT OWN: the operator brain holds 5 bare `P2`, 2 bare `P1` and 1 `P4-Trivial` out of 1,824 rows, measured read-only on 2026-08-04. **TD-338 SHIPPED, AND ITS FORENSICS REFUTED THE INFERENCE THIS SENTENCE USED TO MAKE.** The old text reasoned that because `normalizePriority` folds a two-character `P2` by construction and schema v18 folded the stored ones, the rows must have arrived through SYNC. Read-only evidence says otherwise: the `sync_state` pull cursor for the old remote last advanced more than twelve hours BEFORE the earliest dirty row's `updated_at` and only advances when a pull DELIVERS rows for the table; `sync_queue` records a PUSH of one of those rows ten minutes after its own `updated_at`; and the VPS today holds the CANONICAL spelling for five of them. The rows were BORN LOCALLY through a writer that did not normalise, and travelled OUT. TD-338 nonetheless closed the ingress gap — it is a code fact in both `mergeRows` copies, independent of these rows — as a PREVENTION, and folded these seven in schema v24. `P4-Trivial` is deliberately NOT folded and NOT adopted: see the change procedure. FR-247 does NOT fold them: the picker renders a non-canonical CURRENT value as a DISABLED *not offerable* entry so a brief never silently looks unset, the list badge renders it verbatim, and the read FILTER's options stay data-derived so it remains filterable. G6 both-directions bypass independence) | TD-150 (brief gate) / PI-004 (phase guard) / TD-325 (AC gate) / FR-267 (event gate) | Renaming a bypass var silently disables an escape hatch — sweep the hook + hook-doc + both test suites. These are one-shot env vars; never `export` them. **`IGRIS_BYPASS_AC_GATE` is deliberately NOT in the pre-commit phase-guard block** — that whole block is wrapped in `IGRIS_BYPASS_PHASE_GUARD != 1`, and the orchestrator sets that flag on the exact closing commit the AC gate must inspect, so a check placed there would be bypassed by construction on every close. The two hooks stay separate for that reason. **A bypass of one `commit-msg` gate must never silence the other:** since FR-267 the hook has a shared prelude and per-section skips (`IGRIS_BYPASS_AC_GATE` skips §2 only, `IGRIS_BYPASS_EVENT_GATE` skips §3 only) — before it, the AC bypass was a bare `exit 0`, which would have disabled the event gate by construction. Never `export` either. |

NO SHIPPED RECORD WAS EVER CORRUPTED: the 7.2.1 entry was cut on a hotfix branch off v7.2.0 and carries the correct list, and and the 7.0.0 entry's bypass line in this branch's CHANGELOG is uncorrupted (cited by heading not line: `## [Unreleased]` sits above it, so every future entry shifts its line number — same reason row 65 moved to symbols). `/boot` exceptions only, `/scan` the full roster)<br>`core/skills/{boot,scan}/SKILL.md` runtime mirrors `~/.igris/core/skills/{boot,scan}/SKILL.md` (TD-096)<br>`docs/COGNITION.md` (the prose account: per-instance job, gate, driver, output, and TD-423's `produced` line per instance) | FR-118 / FR-211 / FR-119 / FR-116 / TD-327 | The registry is OPEN, not a closed `'perception'|'subconscious'` enum: a NEW extractor is added by dropping a self-describing `cognition/extractors/<name>.ts` (declares `buildContext`/`promptBuilder`/`parseResponse`/`persistCandidate`/`config`/`id`) — the engine `runExtractor(instance, args)` runs it with ZERO engine edit. Adding an instance must NOT require touching `cognition/engine/`; if it does, the abstraction has leaked — fix the engine, not the instance. FR-211 added `synapse.ts` (edge inference) and FR-119 added `janitor.ts` (near-dupe merge) this exact way: one `extractors/index.ts` barrel line + `createSynapseComponent()`/`createJanitorComponent()` composed into `createCognitionComponent`, engine untouched. The synapse + janitor component-wrappers live at top-level `brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/{synapse,janitor}/` (mirroring `components/subconscious/`, scanned event-bus dirs) — the instance FILES stay under `cognition/extractors/`. FR-116 M2 added `arbiter.ts` (contradiction resolution), FR-116 M3 added `curator.ts` (outdated pruning), and FR-116 M4 added `cartographer.ts` (cluster meta-learning) with ONLY a barrel line + component-internal modules at `brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/{arbiter,curator,cartographer}/` — NO new component wrapper/cron/flag: all three are CO-SCHEDULED by the janitor runner (`janitor/runner.ts` drives them after the near-dupe extractor) under the single `cognition.janitor.enabled` flag (Decision #4A), engine + registry untouched. The cartographer additionally rides the `cognition.janitor.cluster.enabled` sub-toggle (DEFAULT OFF) + a `cadence_days` throttle (Leiden is expensive). **TD-327 adds two obligations.** (1) A new instance MUST declare a `health` block — event `component` and `event_prefix` LITERALS, the `gate_keys` conjunction, the `driver`/`driver_ref`, and the output destination. It is a REQUIRED field on `CognitionInstance`, so `tsc` enumerates every site; an instance that cannot say how an operator sees it stop would otherwise ship invisible. (2) The roster is PROJECTED from `registry.all()` and NEVER hand-listed, in the brain, in the CLI, or in either skill. A hand-list over an OPEN registry cannot report on the members nobody remembered to list — that is the defect TD-327 exists to close, and re-introducing one anywhere in this chain re-opens it. Declare event namespaces as LITERALS: `perception` writes under the bare `perception` component, NOT `cognition.perception`, so a derived `cognition.${id}` silently omits it. **TD-423 adds a THIRD obligation.** (3) A new instance MUST also declare `produced` — the IDENTITY predicate naming which rows are attributable to it, in the grammar `table[col='literal']` or `table[col=literal, col2=OTHER]`. It is REQUIRED for the same reason `health` is, and it is DISTINCT from `output`: `output` is legitimately a STATE predicate (perception declares its review INBOX, which selects zero rows on a drained queue) while `produced` is an identity one, so an instance that declares only `output` is unscoreable and, worse, scoreable WRONG. The bare token `OTHER` means the complement of every literal any other roster row declares for the same table and column, resolved from the ROSTER by the reader — never a list in the CLI. `roster.test.ts`'s completeness gate requires a non-empty, parseable `produced` for every registered instance, so an instance that ships without one is red in the brain suite rather than permanently `unmeasured` three packages away. |

PROPOSAL-ONLY, READ-ONLY over `entity_edges`, never mutates `VALID_EDGE_TYPES`)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/janitor/schema.ts` (v1 `brain_maintenance_runs` + `learnings` ALTER; v2 contradiction counters + `superseded_by`; v3/FR-116 M3 `brain_maintenance_undo` table + `outdated_proposed`/`outdated_pruned`/`undone` counters + `learnings.last_reviewed_at`; v4/FR-116 M4 `clusters_detected`/`meta_learnings_created` counters; v5/FR-116 M5 `edge_types_proposed` counter)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/janitor/hygiene.ts` (`detectOutdatedLearnings` staleness detector; `applyConfidenceBumps` ALSO logs undo entries)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/janitor/runner.ts` (writes `brain_maintenance_runs`; co-drives arbiter + curator + cartographer + runs the M5 emergence sweep + aggregates their counters into the SAME row)<br>ALL `review_status='approved'` readers (`brain-mcp-server/src/tools/memory.ts` x10, `brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/subconscious/digest.ts:248`, `brain-mcp-server/src/tools/sync.ts:952`) auto-exclude `'merged'`/`'superseded'`/`'pruned'` — NO edit needed (Decision A / FR-116 Decision #1) | FR-119 / FR-116 | `merge_learnings` + `resolve_contradiction` + `prune_learning` (prune verdict) are the apply-actions that remove a learning from recall (soft-delete via `review_status='merged'`/`'superseded'`/`'pruned'` — recall/search/sync gate on `='approved'`, so ZERO read-path sweep). `cluster_meta` is the only kind that CREATES a learning (a synthesized meta) + wires `cluster_member_of` edges member→meta via `handleEdgeCreate` (#206) — its undo entry (`action_kind='cluster_meta'`, `learning_id`=the new meta id) reverses by DELETING the meta + its edges (the only CREATE-reversing undo branch in `performUndo`). `deleted_at`/`merged_into`/`superseded_by`/`last_reviewed_at` are AUDIT-ONLY, never a recall gate. Each PRODUCER (persist slots + auto forks) and its CONSUMER MUST keep the `suggested_action` param shape identical (the synapse↔add_edge lesson). `brain_maintenance_runs` is created under the `'janitor'` migration key; FR-116 M2 SHARES it via a v2 ALTER, M3 via a v3 ALTER, M4 via a v4 ALTER. FR-116 M3 also retrofits `applyMergeLearnings`/`applyResolveContradiction`/`applyConfidenceBumps` to ALSO write `brain_maintenance_undo` pre-state rows (additive — behavior + contracts unchanged; the write is fail-soft so a brain without v3 simply records nothing). Re-derive rather than round. THE TOTAL CEILING IS NOT OPTIONAL: without it `React.lazy` is an unbounded way to spend bytes behind a boundary the initial ceiling cannot see. DEMONSTRATED — plant B put 40 KB behind the lazy Graph route: INITIAL stayed green at 285_390 B and TOTAL went red by 16_017 B. A plant must assign the WHOLE value (`window.__bulk = BULK`), not a foldable property of it — `BULK.length` constant-folds to a number and the literal is tree-shaken, which silently produces a plant that plants nothing. |

No brain source was edited by FR-249: every tool it names was already registered, so the by-hand `npm test` obligation at the end of this row is informational for that brief rather than triggered. Each row carries `bulk`, a `target`, an `extra` ALLOW-LIST, and then EITHER an `idKey` or a `refKeys` map plus optional `fixed` and `rename` blocks. The `idKey` is ASYMMETRIC on purpose — the three suggestion tools declare `required: ['id']` and the two perception tools declare `required: ['learning_id']` — so getting it wrong is a gateway REFUSAL rather than a silent mis-write. `apply` is the one `bulk: false` row (D4: it dispatches arbitrary action KINDS, and bulk-firing heterogeneous side effects behind one confirm is not a triage flow). `extra` is an allow-list rather than documentation: `buildTriageArgs` copies ONLY the keys its row names, so a body posting `{reason, brief_id, winner_id}` at `dismiss` cannot reach the handler with `winner_id`. FR-247 WIDENED THE ROW SHAPE ONCE, ADDITIVELY, AND ADDED NO ENDPOINT. `target` is `id`, `brief-ref` or — since FR-249 — `none`: the five FR-241 rows address a row by INTEGER id, but a brief is the `(project, brief_id)` PAIR and no brain tool accepts `brief_status.id`, so the request body gained `refs` beside `ids` while the path set stayed sixteen GET and one POST **at FR-247** (count:record FR-247) — asserted byte-identically at the time against row 109's exact-set string and `SMOKE_PROBE_PATHS`. (Past tense: FR-248 and FR-266 have both added a path since, and both instruments moved with them each time. FR-247's point — a mutation that needs no new path must not take one — is unaffected. THE LIVE COUNT IS NOT THIS ROW'S TO CARRY: it lives in row 110, and this parenthetical previously restated it, went stale at FR-266, and survived three sweeps because it SHOUTED the number in caps while every sweep grepped lower-case. Cite row 110; do not mirror it.) `refKeys` states per row WHICH halves of the ref reach the tool: `set_priority` forwards both, and SINCE BR-083 `attach_goal` forwards both too. It forwarded `brief_id` ALONE until then, and that absence was the BR-078 asymmetry written down where the ambiguity was minted, because `entity_edges.from_id` was the bare brief id and `getGoal`'s serving-briefs join carried no project predicate. BR-083 CLOSED IT: the column exists, the ref's `project` reaches the tool as `from_project`, and the forward is now load-bearing rather than cosmetic — `handleEdgeCreate` REFUSES an ambiguous endpoint, so dropping the project half would turn a silently-wrong edge into a hard refusal. `fixed` pins constants the MAP supplies and the caller never can — `attach_goal` pins `from_type` brief, `to_type` goal and `edge_type` serves_goal, because a caller-supplied `edge_type` would silently turn ONE row into about twenty mutations. THE TD-311 BOUNDARY IS PART OF THIS CONTRACT, AND SINCE FR-249 IT IS SCOPED TO AN ENTITY: no row may name `status`, `phase`, `content`, `title` or `filename` in `extra` or `fixed`, and no `rename` may TARGET one, FOR A ROW WHOSE TOOL WRITES A BRIEF. That ban was GLOBAL and entity-blind until FR-249, which is why it structurally refused goal creation in all three of the shapes that brief considered: it could not tell a goal's title from a brief's. TD-311's invariant is a claim about BRIEFS, and a goal is a different table with no `/hunt` state machine over it. THE WIDENING WAS RATIFIED BY THE OPERATOR RATHER THAN NOTICED IN A DIFF, and it is only as good as three clauses, each of which has its own assertion: the tool-to-entity table is TOTAL over the map, so a row whose tool has no classification REDS rather than being silently exempt; it FAILS CLOSED, so an unclassified tool is reported AND still checked against the brief ban; and a PLANTED brief-title write must still be refused, because a control that only proves the predicate fires cannot prove it stopped firing on purpose. ADDING A ROW THEREFORE MEANS CLASSIFYING ITS TOOL, not merely naming it. `igris_brief_sync` is deliberately absent from that table — it is forbidden by name, so recording an entity for it would be classifying a tool no row may dispatch, and the fail-closed default reports it twice instead; `igris_brief_sync` is forbidden BY NAME, because it declares `title` and `status` REQUIRED and therefore overwrites both on every call, so no row using it could express a partial write. `dashboard-server.test.ts` asserts all of that at RUNTIME over the frozen object, with a self-negative-control that runs the same predicate over a deliberately dirty map. ONE ROW IS ONE DISPATCH: no row may fire two tools or thread one tool's output into another's input. FR-249 WAS THE FIRST BRIEF TO TEST THAT RULE AND IT SURVIVED VERBATIM — goal creation shipped as its OWN simple row and the operator attaches with a second click, so the map is still eight rows, eight tools, eight dispatches. The COMPOSITE was costed and REJECTED on three grounds and not on appetite: it deletes this rule, it sweeps six assertions over the most-asserted frozen object in this tier, and it buys an acceptance criterion that CANNOT BE TESTED WITHOUT A MOCK, because `igris_edge_create` never verifies the goal exists (an INSERT OR IGNORE on any to_id) so an attach cannot fail on a goal that was just created. If one click is ever wanted it is a brief that argues this rule on its own terms. FR-249 ADDED THE THIRD TARGET KIND AND ONE NEW FIELD, both additively. `target: 'none'` is a mutation with NO SUBJECT: `create_goal` is the first row that addresses nothing, both `ids` and `refs` are refused BY NAME for it, its requested count is 1 rather than the sum of two empty arrays (without which a successful create reports requested 0 and applied 1), and the anti-vacuity gate that refuses an empty `ids` does not apply and cannot, since a subjectless create is never vacuous. `returns` is a DOTTED PATH INTO THE TOOL'S OWN JSON RESULT, declared on the row and walked generically by `dispatchSubjectless`, and it is the ONLY fragment of any tool payload that crosses back to the client — `create_goal` declares `goal.goal_id` so the browser can preselect the goal it just made, and the other seven rows declare none and report a null. A `returns` path must never also appear in `extra` or `fixed`: it is a READ, and a row where the same name is both would be doing two things through one key. THE WIRE KEYS FOR THAT ROW ARE PREFIXED, AND THAT IS A SECOND LAYER RATHER THAN A DUPLICATE OF THE FIRST: `parseTriageBody`'s unknown-key set is GLOBAL, so admitting a bare `title` would stop `title` being refused BY ABSENCE for `set_priority` too — the body therefore says `goal_title`, `goal_outcome` and `goal_project`, and the row's `rename` turns them into the tool's argument names at the boundary. THE RULE THIS ENCODES: a dashboard mutation may only ever be added by adding a row to this map, and a mutation that does not resolve to a registered brain tool is forbidden — which is what makes *no raw SQL in the server layer* assertable BY CONSTRUCTION rather than by scan, because there is no other way for this tier to write. | The **`briefs_fts` lexical index, its six triggers and schema v23** (FR-246): a CONTENTLESS fts5 table (`content=''`, `contentless_delete=1`) keyed `rowid = brief_status.id` over `brief_id` + `title` + `brief_files.content`, created by `db.ts`'s v23 block together with three triggers on `brief_status` (AI/AU/AD) and three on `brief_files` (AI/AU/AD). WHY IT EXISTS: before v23 the ONLY retrieval over briefs was `briefs_vec`, and that index is thinner than it looks — `extractBriefProblem` embeds the title plus the `## Problem` section only (falling back to the first 500 characters), it is called at CREATE and by the backfill tool and NOWHERE ELSE, and the only trigger on it is `briefs_vec_ad` (DELETE). So a brief's BODY was not searchable at all and an EDITED brief carries a STALE vector. `briefs_fts` is therefore not merely the offline fallback for the vector arm — it is the ONLY arm that reaches `brief_files.content`, and the only one that is current after an edit. Whether to re-embed on update is a SEPARATE brief and is deliberately NOT fixed here. CONTENTLESS is a MEASURED decision, not a preference: built both ways on a `VACUUM INTO` snapshot of the operator brain (1,814 `brief_status` rows, 1,597 `brief_files` rows totalling 6,211,271 bytes of content), a contentful fts5 cost +11,452,416 B and this one +3,846,144 B, and the reader needs only `rowid` and `rank` because it hydrates every displayed column from `brief_status`. The floor for `contentless_delete=1` is SQLite 3.43; both packages pin `better-sqlite3: ^11.0.0`, whose oldest member bundles 3.45.3. THE SHARP EDGE, invisible in the DDL: a contentless fts5 does NOT reject an INSERT for a rowid it already holds — it appends a second index entry and a later MATCH can return that rowid twice — so EVERY trigger is DELETE-then-INSERT, never a bare INSERT, and the backfill runs once into a table created in the same transaction. The `brief_files` triggers resolve the rowid by subquery against `brief_status` and use `INSERT ... SELECT`, which self-skips when that subquery is empty: `handleBriefCreate` writes `brief_files` FIRST and `brief_status` SECOND in one transaction, so at file-insert time the status row may not exist yet. v23 is ADDITIVE and touches no existing row, and it NEVER bumps `updated_at` (an LWW sync column) because it issues no UPDATE at all; it nonetheless takes and VERIFIES a `.pre-v23.bak` snapshot (`PRAGMA integrity_check` plus a `brief_status` row-count match) and ABORTS at v22 if that snapshot cannot be verified, by FR-246 operator sign-off. A failed v23 is NOT fatal: `schema_version` stays at 22 and the reader reports `bm25_reason: 'brain table absent: briefs_fts (schema v23 not applied)'`. `briefs_fts` is a LOCAL DERIVED index and is NOT in `SYNC_TABLES`. | `protocol` | `brain-mcp-server/src/db.ts` (**OWNER** — the v23 DDL, the six triggers and the backfill)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/tools/briefs-read.ts` (`hybridSearchBriefs` — the ONLY reader; it preflights the table and reports `bm25_reason` rather than throwing)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/tools/briefs.ts` (INDIRECT WRITERS whose statements the triggers must fire on — the `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` pair in `handleBriefCreate` and the two plain UPDATEs in the status/phase handlers)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/tools/sync.ts` (the remaining indirect writers — the `ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` upsert and `mergeRows`' plain INSERT/UPDATE. The failure this guards is the G-01R class: a write path that does not fire the triggers leaves the index silently stale, and a stale FTS index is indistinguishable from a search that legitimately matched nothing. Never convert a trigger to a bare INSERT (see the duplicate-rowid note above) and never re-add `INSERT OR REPLACE` on either source table. | **Replication INGRESS is a normalization boundary** (TD-338): every inbound sync row for a table in `SYNC_NORMALIZED_FIELDS` passes through the SAME write-boundary normalizers before it is stored, in BOTH `mergeRows` copies. FOLD-KNOWN, PASSTHROUGH-UNKNOWN, REPORT-BOTH. Two doors must stay closed: the brain's (reached from `handleBrainPull` AND `processSyncPush`, the body of `POST /sync/push`) and the CLI's (`mergePulledTables`, the awaken and `igris boot-sync` VPS→local pull) — and **the CLI door is the one that runs on a workstation**, so a brain-only change closes the door nobody walks through. The LWW `timestampCol` is DELIBERATELY absent from the map: that absence is what makes the fold non-oscillating (no merge path in either package writes a timestamp it did not receive, so a folded row produces no delta with a newer timestamp, and the fixed point is reached on the first arrival of each row version). The steady state is a silent CONTENT divergence at equal timestamps, which is inert for sync and is the deliberate price of keeping LWW honest. Folding AND bumping `updated_at` would heal an un-migrated remote and is REJECTED, recorded as a lever rather than an oversight. `igris import` (FR-230) is a DELIBERATE NON-CONSUMER: folding at the import writer would make the stored row differ from the bundle's `rowContentHash`, so a re-import would classify it as modified-locally against its own ancestor — a spurious conflict in the import ledger, which deserves its own decision. | `protocol` | `brain-mcp-server/src/tools/sync.ts` (`mergeRows` — the brain door; The union, the dispatch and the emission order ARE derived; the DATA layer is hand-listed, and a body referencing a table the renderer never emitted produces a mirror that does not compile. The mirror's `SyncNormalizerId` union and dispatch table are DERIVED from the map and need no edit, and a mapped id with no authored body THROWS at generation time naming what to add. Every authored function in the emitted module has a fixture table replayed CLI-side (`NORMALIZE_FIXTURES`, `PREDICATE_FIXTURES`, `SYNC_ROW_FIXTURES`); adding a fourth authored function without its fixture table silently re-opens the logic-drift gap byte-parity cannot see. The REPLAY DISPATCH on both sides is a map with a completeness assertion against `normalizerIdsInUse`, NOT a fallback chain: before TD-333 both were chains whose final `else` was the phase normalizer, so a fourth id's fixtures would have been replayed through the wrong function — do not reintroduce a fallback arm. |

It is charged against `TOTAL_JS_CEILING`, never the initial set, so the BYTE argument is much weaker post-split than the pre-split '484 B of slack' version of this sentence claimed. | The **`entity_edges` project-qualification storage contract** (BR-083): `from_project` and `to_project`, both NULLABLE, added by migration `edges:4` through a 12-step table rebuild. THREE clauses, and each one is load-bearing. ONE — NULL means DELIBERATELY UNATTRIBUTED, never "unknown, guess later": the backfill attributes a row only when `resolveEdgeProjects` already rates it `unique`, and 327 of 785 live rows (41.7 percent) stay NULL forever because a wrong attribution is worse than a null. Never fill a NULL from an inference the shipped resolver does not rate unique; widening the provable classes requires a brief. TWO — the requirement rule is CONDITIONAL and lives in `handleEdgeCreate`, which is a choke point rather than a per-tool guard. A qualifier is required iff the endpoint id exists in more than one project, computed empirically by `projectsFor` and never from a type list, so `PROJECT_SCOPED_TYPES` stays a doc-constant no code branches on. It cannot be a JSON-Schema `required` entry (there is no conditional required, and a blanket one would reject the legitimately project-less concept and synapse edges) and it cannot be a DDL CHECK (it cannot read `brief_status`). `handleEdgeCreate` is where every writer passes: measured, ONE gateway-dispatched call site plus NINE in-process callers that never touch dispatch. The BR-083 plan said EIGHT and missed the synapse auto-approve path — re-derive that set with a grep, never from a list. The error strings are BR-078 wording by CONSTRUCTION, not by copy: `qualifyNodeProject` is the single ladder both surfaces call, and the only difference is a noun and a trailer. THREE — the UNIQUE constraint is an EXPRESSION INDEX over `COALESCE(from_project, empty)` and `COALESCE(to_project, empty)`, because NULL is DISTINCT from NULL in a SQLite UNIQUE and a table-level form would let two identical project-less concept edges both insert, silently breaking idempotency for exactly the population that legitimately has no project. A table-level UNIQUE also cannot be dropped in place, which is why `edges:4` REBUILDS — and the rebuild is what makes two edges differing ONLY by project storable at all, a defect no read-path fix could reach. a false or throwing pre-flight ABORTS the migration and every later one for that component, so the next boot retries)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/edges/handlers.ts` (the ladder, the INSERT, and the idempotency read-back — which must match on both projects with IS rather than equals, or two edges differing only by project read back each other row)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/edges/node-project.ts` (`qualifyNodeProject`, `describeCandidates`, `hintedQualifier` and the stored-projects hop branch)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/edges/index.ts` (both `properties` sets — `required` is NOT touched — plus the two in-process hooks, where the event payload project is an ASSERTION on the near side and only a HINT on the far side)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/edges/whole-graph.ts` (branch 0 and `loadGraphInputs`)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/edges/traversal.ts` (the probed project columns and the orientation swap)<br>`brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/goals/read.ts` and `brain-mcp-server/src/engine/components/goals/handlers.ts` (five joins gained the predicate; Each tier's failure falls to the NEXT tier WITH ONE DELIBERATE EXCEPTION, and the exception is the whole reason the override is worth keeping: an override that PARSES but names a dead pid returns `null` without running the walk, because substituting a walked pid for a pid the operator deliberately named is itself a wrong-but-plausible answer. | The **MCP Streamable HTTP wire contract on POST /mcp** (BR-094): the outbound Accept header must name BOTH media types — the SDK transport substring-tests the raw header for each one independently and answers 406 with a JSON-RPC envelope BEFORE any dispatch — and the response body arrives in one of TWO shapes chosen by whether the brain currently holds an active MCP session: plain JSON from the no-session direct-dispatch fallback, or a Server-Sent-Events frame from the transport, which never answers a POST with JSON because `enableJsonResponse` is left at its default of false. THREE halves, then, and each fails differently: without the header the call is refused at 406; with the header but a JSON-only reader the call SUCCEEDS remotely and reads as unreadable locally; and with an SSE reader whose JSON-RPC id is a module constant rather than fresh per call, the call reads a CO-TENANT run answer as its own. That third one is a data-loss path, not a cosmetic one: the SDK transport demultiplexes replies by JSON-RPC id per transport, the brain funnels every session-less POST into a single injected session, and SSE is only reachable when such a session exists — so the id MUST be per-call unique and the reader MUST also refuse a frame that is not a JSON-RPC response at all. | `protocol` | `cli/src/lib/mcp-client.ts` (sends the header; `newMcpRequestId` mints the per-call uuid; `readMcpResponseBody` selects the reader from the response content type and `readSseJsonRpc` accepts a frame only if it is a non-array object carrying `jsonrpc` 2.0, one of result or error, AND that same id. Dropping either media token restores the 406; deleting the content-type branch restores an HTTP 200 whose body no reader can interpret; making the id a constant again re-opens BR-080 loss class through the correlation itself. NEVER hardcode a JSON-RPC id in a fixture on this path — pass `rpcRequestId(call)`. Verify against the LIVE remote and not only against the loopback: the loopback validates nothing, and the entire unit suite was green for the whole time every real drain was 406ing. ## Citation conventions

What `scripts/check_contract_consumers.sh` does and does not validate in the
**Consumers** column (TD-334; run `scripts/check_contract_consumers.sh --help`
for the authoritative version — this section is the author-facing summary).

**Every backticked token in a Consumers cell is classified.** A token is treated
as a repo-path citation, and therefore hard-fails when it does not resolve, when
it contains a `/`, uses only the characters `A-Za-z0-9_./*{},+-`, is not rooted
at `~` or `/`, and does not start with `..`. Everything else is skipped **and
counted** — the run prints `map citations: N validated / M skipped`, so an exit 0
never reads as "the whole map is healthy".

| You write | The checker does |
|---|---|
| `cli/src/lib/paths.ts` | resolves it (repo root first, then as a path suffix of a tracked file) |
| `pages/Graph.tsx` | resolves it as a suffix — short forms relative to a directory the row's prose establishes are a supported convention |
| `cli/src/lib/paths.ts:148` | resolves the path AND requires line 148 to exist; ranges (`:79-102`) and lists (`:900,1359`) are checked number by number |
| `core/skills/*/SKILL.md`, `core/os/{conduct,standards}.md` | expands it; an empty expansion or a missing brace member is a hard-fail. A trailing `/**` is validated as its directory (`**` is not a bash-3.2 pattern). An INTERIOR `**` (e.g. `a/**/b`) is narrowed to a single `*`, matching one segment rather than any depth — so a legitimate citation nested two or more levels deep would HARD-FAIL. None exists today; lengthen or split such a citation rather than loosening the resolver |
| `cli/dist/lib/harness-manifest.json` | skips it — git ignores it, so it is absent on a clean checkout |
| `buildBrainGraph(db, opts)`, `$defs/surface_contract`, `<harness>.md`, `handlers.ts:NN` | skips it — not path-shaped, so it is prose, a signature, a schema pointer or a placeholder |
| `index.ts`, `db.js` | skips it — a slash-less token cannot be told apart from an identifier |
| `~/.igris/core/skills/boot/SKILL.md` | skips it — a runtime reader, not a repo file |
| `../../../db.js` | skips it — a module-relative import specifier, not a citation |

**Two things to know before you author a citation:**

1. **A path-shaped token that is NOT a file will hard-fail the commit.** If you
   want to name a slash-joined thing that is not a repo path — an npm module
   specifier, a slash-joined enumeration of event names, a tarball member — write
   it un-backticked, or split it into separate backticked tokens. A doc in
   ANOTHER repository is written `<repo>:<path>`, e.g.
   `fifty_dev:docs/brand/dataviz.md`; the colon-plus-non-digits form is skipped.
2. **Line numbers are validated in two tiers.** A number past the end of the file
   is a hard-fail. A line that exists but is blank or a bare closing delimiter is
   a WARNING (exit 0) — "points at a construct" is a cheap proxy, not a proof.
   The map's baseline is zero warnings, so any warning you see is yours.

**What a default (staged) run proves.** The pre-commit hook runs the map check
ONLY when `MAINTAINING.md` is itself staged. That is right at commit time (the
map WILL be staged in a commit that edits it), but it means an exit 0 from an
interactive pre-commit run says nothing about map health. `--paths` mode always
validates — that is the invocation to reach for when you want the answer.
